    Turns auto-sync off, so nothing syncs unless someone runs it. Do it before a rollback or a manual fix, so the controller does not overwrite your change. Turn it on again with --sync-policy automated. If the app comes from an app of apps with selfHeal, or from an ApplicationSet, the parent puts the policy back within seconds: disable auto-sync on the parent first.

    Changes state

    argocd --core app set APP --sync-policy automated --self-heal --auto-prune

    Syncs by itself on every Git change, reverts manual edits (--self-heal) and deletes what Git no longer has (--auto-prune). A wrong commit or path is then applied and pruned at once. Add the flags one at a time. An app owned by a parent app or an ApplicationSet gets this reverted unless you change the parent.

    Can lose data

    Argo CD: OutOfSync and drift

    kubectl -n argocd patch app APP --type json -p '[{"op":"add","path":"/spec/ignoreDifferences","value":[{"group":"apps","kind":"Deployment","jsonPointers":["/spec/replicas"]}]}]'

    Starts an ignore list: Argo CD ignores spec.replicas of Deployments in this app, so after the next refresh the app is Synced even when live replicas differ. This replaces any existing list. A Git-managed Application (app of apps, ApplicationSet) is changed in Git, or the parent reverts it. A sync still writes the Git value unless the RespectIgnoreDifferences=true sync option is set.

    Changes state

    kubectl -n argocd patch app APP --type json -p '[{"op":"add","path":"/spec/ignoreDifferences/-","value":{"group":"apps","kind":"Deployment","jsonPointers":["/spec/replicas"]}}]'

    Appends one rule to an existing ignoreDifferences list. It fails with The request is invalid when the app has no list yet: use the entry above to create it. A --type merge patch would replace the whole list.

    Changes state

    argocd --core app get APP --refresh | grep -E 'Sync Status|OutOfSync'

    After you change an ignore rule or a webhook, refresh and check that the rows leave OutOfSync. A mutating webhook, or an admission policy, that rewrites a field Git sets shows as a permanent diff with < as the mutated live value.

    Argo CD: stuck deletion and app of apps

    kubectl -n argocd get app APP -o jsonpath='{.metadata.deletionTimestamp}{" "}{.metadata.finalizers}{"\n"}'

    A timestamp with resources-finalizer.argocd.argoproj.io still listed means Argo CD is waiting to delete the app's objects. It stays there until every object is gone. argocd app get APP shows which resource is Progressing.

    kubectl -n NS get configmap CMNAME -o jsonpath='{.metadata.deletionTimestamp}{" "}{.metadata.finalizers}{"\n"}'

    Checks a stuck object, here a ConfigMap but any kind works: a deletionTimestamp with a finalizer of another controller is what blocks the app delete. Fix that controller, or remove the finalizer by hand if its owner is gone.

    kubectl -n argocd patch app APP --type merge -p '{"metadata":{"finalizers":null}}'

    Removes the Argo CD finalizer so the Application object disappears. The app's live objects are not deleted: they stay in the cluster, unmanaged. Use it after the blocking object is cleared or when the destination cluster no longer exists. A parent app of apps with selfHeal, or an ApplicationSet, puts the finalizer back: disable auto-sync on the parent first.

    Can lose data

    argocd --core app delete APP --yes

    Deletes the Application and, by default, every object it created. The CLI returns application 'APP' deleted. Add --cascade=false to remove only the Application and leave the objects running.

    Can lose data

    argocd --core app resources APP

    For an app of apps: the child Applications are listed as resources of kind Application. The root app can be Synced and Healthy while a child has a ComparisonError, so check argocd --core app list for the children, not only the root.

    Argo CD errors

    ComparisonError Failed to load target state: failed to generate manifest for source 1 of 1: rpc error: code = Unknown desc = nosuchdir: app path does not exist

    The path in the Application is not in the repository at that revision. Check it with git ls-tree. After a repo restructure, update spec.source.path. The app shows sync Unknown.

    ComparisonError Failed to load target state: failed to generate manifest for source 1 of 1: rpc error: code = Unknown desc = unable to resolve 'nosuchbranch' to a commit SHA: revision not found

    targetRevision is not a branch, tag or commit in the repository. Check spelling and that the branch was pushed. A deleted branch causes this after a merge.

    ComparisonError Failed to load target state: failed to generate manifest for source 1 of 1: rpc error: code = FailedPrecondition desc = Failed to unmarshal "app.yaml": failed to unmarshal manifest: error converting YAML to JSON: yaml: line 5: did not find expected key

    A YAML file in the app path does not parse. The message names the file and the line. Fix it in Git; the app recovers on the next refresh.

    ComparisonError Failed to load target state: failed to generate manifest for source 1 of 1: rpc error: code = Unknown desc = failed to execute helm template command: failed running helm: `helm template . --name-template helmapp --namespace helmapp --kube-version 1.37.0 <api versions removed> --include-crds` failed exit status 1: Error: demo/templates/nil.yaml:1:10

    A Helm chart fails to render in the repo-server. The text after Error: is Helm's own error, as helm template prints it. Run the same command locally with the app's values. Fix the chart.

    ComparisonError Failed to load target state: failed to generate manifest for source 1 of 1: rpc error: code = Unknown desc = failed to list refs: Get "http://nosuch.invalid/repo.git/info/refs?service=git-upload-pack": dial tcp: lookup nosuch.invalid on 10.96.0.10:53: no such host

    The repo-server cannot reach the Git host: wrong URL, DNS in the cluster, a firewall or proxy. Test from the pod: kubectl -n argocd exec deploy/argocd-repo-server -- git ls-remote URL. repository not found: at the end means the host answered but the path is wrong.

    ComparisonError Failed to load target state: failed to generate manifest for source 1 of 1: rpc error: code = Unknown desc = failed to list refs: unexpected EOF

    The URL answers, but not as a Git smart-HTTP server. Here it was a static web server that held a bare repository. Argo CD needs a real Git server such as GitHub, GitLab, Gitea or git http-backend.

    one or more objects failed to apply, reason: Service "badsvc" is invalid: [spec.ports[0].port: Invalid value: 99999: must be between 1 and 65535, inclusive, spec.ports[0].targetPort: Invalid value: 99999: must be between 1 and 65535, inclusive]

    The API server rejected an object during sync. The reason after reason: is the Kubernetes validation error, per resource in the table too. Fix the manifest in Git. The operation phase is Failed and the app stays OutOfSync.

    ComparisonError Failed to load target state: failed to generate manifest for source 1 of 1: rpc error: code = Unknown desc = failed to list refs: authentication required: <html>

    The repository needs credentials and Argo CD has none for this URL; the web server's own reply (here a 401 page) follows. Register the repository with argocd repo add URL (add --username and let it prompt for the password) or a repository Secret in the argocd namespace. Check argocd --core repo list.

    one or more objects failed to apply, reason: error when patching "/dev/shm/695332104": Deployment.apps "plain-web" is invalid: spec.selector: Invalid value: {"matchLabels":{"app":"plain","tier":"web"}}: field is immutable

    A manifest in Git changed an immutable field, usually a Deployment spec.selector. The sync fails and the app stays OutOfSync. Revert it, or delete the object by hand and sync again, accepting downtime. The temp file name differs each time.

    one or more objects failed to apply, reason: namespaces "nons-missing" not found

    The destination namespace does not exist and the app does not create it. Add the sync option CreateNamespace=true to the Application or create the namespace first, then sync again.

    one or more synchronization tasks completed unsuccessfully

    A sync hook failed, here a PreSync Job that exited 1 (the table row says Job has reached the specified backoff limit). Read the Job's Pod logs in the app's namespace. The main resources were not applied. Fix the hook and sync again.

    waiting for completion of hook batch/Job/presync-hang

    The operation is Running because a hook Job has not finished. The app can show Synced and Healthy while this lasts. Read the Job's Pod. Stop with argocd app terminate-op APP if it will never end.

    InvalidSpecError: application destination server 'https://kubernetes.default.svc' and namespace 'locked1' do not match any of the allowed destinations in project 'locked'

    The AppProject restricts destinations (and sources: application repo URL is not permitted in project 'NAME'). Add the destination or repo to the project, or move the app to another project. argocd app sync then prints Phase: Error and may keep waiting; press Ctrl-C.

    InvalidSpecError: there are no clusters with this name: nosuchcluster

    spec.destination.name is not a registered cluster. Check argocd cluster list. For an app that points at a removed cluster, delete the app after removing the finalizer.

    rpc error: code = InvalidArgument desc = app is not allowed in project "nosuchproj", or the project does not exist

    The Application's spec.project names a project that is missing or that does not allow this app. Check argocd proj list. Create the project or set spec.project to an existing one.

    Cloud: AWS, Google Cloud, Azure

    What usually breaks in a cloud account: the CLI is signed in as the wrong identity, an instance fails a status check, a load balancer marks every target unhealthy, a firewall rule blocks a port, or a quota stops a launch. Run these from a terminal with read access; the changing commands are tagged.

    Who am I

    aws sts get-caller-identity

    The account, the user or role ARN and the user id the CLI is acting as. Run it first: a wrong profile, region or assumed role explains most "it works on my machine" cloud errors.

    aws configure list

    Where the CLI found its profile, keys and region: the Type column reads env, shared-credentials-file, sso or iam-role. Environment variables win over the profile you thought you chose.

    gcloud auth list gcloud config list

    The active account is marked with *. config list shows the default project, region and zone every command will use. With no account it prints "No credentialed accounts."

    az account show

    The subscription, tenant and signed-in user. A command that finds nothing is often run against the wrong subscription: list them with az account list -o table and switch with az account set --subscription and its name or id.

    AWS: instances and load balancers

    aws ec2 describe-instances --instance-ids INSTANCE --region REGION --query 'Reservations[].Instances[].[InstanceId,State.Name,StateReason.Message]' --output text

    The state (pending, running, stopping, stopped, terminated) and, for a stop you did not ask for, the reason such as Client.InstanceInitiatedShutdown or Server.SpotInstanceTermination.

    aws ec2 describe-instance-status --instance-ids INSTANCE --include-all-instances --region REGION --query 'InstanceStatuses[].[InstanceState.Name,SystemStatus.Status,InstanceStatus.Status]' --output text

    Two status checks. SystemStatus impaired: the AWS host failed, so stop and start the instance to move it. A stop loses instance-store data and, without an Elastic IP, the public IPv4 address. InstanceStatus impaired: the guest OS is unreachable, so read the console output. Without --include-all-instances, stopped instances are left out.

    aws ec2 get-console-output --instance-id INSTANCE --latest --output text --region REGION

    The last console output, as the serial console saw it: kernel panics, a failed mount in fstab, cloud-init errors. Output lags by a few minutes.

    aws elbv2 describe-target-groups --load-balancer-arn ARN --region REGION

    The target groups behind a load balancer, with the health check path, port and thresholds. Compare the health check path and port with what the app really serves.

    aws elbv2 describe-target-health --target-group-arn ARN --region REGION --query 'TargetHealthDescriptions[].[Target.Id,TargetHealth.State,TargetHealth.Reason]' --output text

    State unhealthy with Reason Target.ResponseCodeMismatch, Target.Timeout or Target.FailedHealthChecks: the app answered wrongly or not at all. State unused with Target.NotInUse: no listener rule forwards to the group, or the target's zone is not enabled on the load balancer. Target.NotRegistered: the target is not in this group.

    aws ec2 describe-instances --instance-ids INSTANCE --region REGION --query 'Reservations[].Instances[].SecurityGroups[].GroupId' --output text aws ec2 describe-security-groups --group-ids SGID --region REGION --query 'SecurityGroups[].IpPermissions'

    The security groups on an instance, then their inbound rules. Security groups only allow: no match means dropped, which a client sees as a timeout. Also check the subnet's network ACL, which has deny rules.

    aws cloudtrail lookup-events --lookup-attributes AttributeKey=EventName,AttributeValue=StopInstances --max-results 5 --region REGION

    Who stopped something and when: each event has a Username and a time. Swap in TerminateInstances, AuthorizeSecurityGroupIngress or ModifyInstanceAttribute. Events appear a few minutes late.

    aws health describe-events --region us-east-1 --filter eventStatusCodes=open

    Open AWS Health events for your account. It needs a Business, Enterprise On-Ramp or Enterprise support plan, otherwise it fails with SubscriptionRequiredException. Without one, read the public status page below.

    aws ec2 reboot-instances --instance-ids INSTANCE --region REGION

    Reboots the guest OS on the same host; it keeps the instance id, addresses and disks. An instance that fails the system status check needs a stop and start instead, which loses instance-store data and, without an Elastic IP, the public IPv4 address.

    Changes state

    AWS: quotas and limits

    aws service-quotas get-service-quota --service-code ec2 --quota-code L-1216C47A --region REGION

    The applied value of "Running On-Demand Standard instances", counted in vCPUs, not instances. Compare it with your usage before a launch fails with VcpuLimitExceeded. Quotas are per region.

    aws service-quotas list-service-quotas --service-code ec2 --region REGION --query 'Quotas[?contains(QuotaName, `Running On-Demand`)].[QuotaName,QuotaCode,Value]' --output text

    Every On-Demand vCPU quota by instance family, with its code for get-service-quota. Request an increase in the Service Quotas console or with aws service-quotas request-service-quota-increase.

    Google Cloud

    gcloud compute instances list --filter="status!=RUNNING"

    Every instance in the default project that is not running: TERMINATED, STOPPING, SUSPENDED or STAGING. A TERMINATED instance you did not stop was preempted or crashed: check the operations list.

    gcloud compute instances describe INSTANCE --zone ZONE --format="value(status,statusMessage)"

    The state, plus a message when the platform stopped the instance. Add --format=yaml for the whole resource: scheduling, network interfaces, service account and metadata.

    gcloud compute instances get-serial-port-output INSTANCE --zone ZONE --start=0

    The serial console log: boot messages, kernel panics, startup script output. Works with no SSH and no network. --start is a byte offset, so re-run with the last offset to read only new output.

    gcloud compute backend-services get-health BACKEND --global

    Health of every backend of a global load balancer, per instance group: HEALTHY or UNHEALTHY. Use --region REGION for a regional one. All unhealthy usually means the health check probe range is blocked: see the firewall entry below.

    gcloud compute firewall-rules list --sort-by=priority

    Rules with their direction, priority, ranges and allowed protocols. Lowest priority number wins. The VPC denies ingress by default, so a load balancer's health checks need an allow rule for Google's published probe ranges.

    gcloud compute instances network-interfaces get-effective-firewalls INSTANCE --zone ZONE

    The firewall rules that really apply to one instance, after policies and inheritance. Use it when the rule list looks right but traffic is still dropped.

    gcloud compute regions describe REGION --format="yaml(quotas)"

    Per-region quotas, each with limit, usage and metric: CPUS, IN_USE_ADDRESSES, SSD_TOTAL_GB. A metric where usage equals the limit is the one blocking creates. Project-wide quotas: gcloud compute project-info describe --format="yaml(quotas)".

    gcloud compute operations list --filter="status!=DONE OR error:*" --sort-by=~insertTime --limit=20

    Compute operations still pending or running, and recent ones that failed (a failed one is still DONE, with error set): live migrations, stops, preemptions.

    gcloud compute instances reset INSTANCE --zone ZONE

    A hard reset, like the power button on a server: no clean shutdown, so open files and database writes can be lost. The instance keeps its disks and IPs. Use gcloud compute instances stop and start when a clean stop works; a stop releases an ephemeral external IP.

    Can lose data

    Azure

    az vm list -g RGNAME -d -o table

    Every VM in a resource group with its PowerState (VM running, VM deallocated, VM stopped) and addresses. A VM stopped machine is still billed; only deallocated releases compute.

    az vm get-instance-view -g RGNAME -n INSTANCE --query "instanceView.statuses[].displayStatus" -o tsv

    Provisioning and power state in plain words, such as Provisioning succeeded and VM running. A Provisioning failed line is followed by the reason in the activity log.

    az vm boot-diagnostics get-boot-log -g RGNAME -n INSTANCE

    The serial console log of the VM. It needs boot diagnostics enabled on the VM. Look for kernel panics, a stuck fstab mount and cloud-init errors.

    az network application-gateway show-backend-health -g RGNAME -n GATEWAY

    Health of each backend server of an Application Gateway: Healthy, Unhealthy or Unknown, with a log message. For a Standard Load Balancer there is no such command: watch the DipAvailability metric in az monitor metrics list.

    az network nsg rule list -g RGNAME --nsg-name NSGNAME -o table

    Rules with priority, direction, access and port ranges. The lowest priority number is evaluated first. The built-in default rules, priority 65000 and above, do not show here: add --include-default.

    az network nic list-effective-nsg -g RGNAME -n NICNAME

    The rules in force on one network interface, merged from the subnet's and the NIC's security groups. The VM must be running. Find the NIC with az vm show -g RGNAME -n INSTANCE --query networkProfile.

    az vm list-usage --location REGION -o table

    CurrentValue against Limit for regional vCPUs and per-family vCPUs. A row at its limit blocks the next create or resize. Raise it from the portal, under Usage + quotas.

    az vm list-skus --location REGION --size Standard_D2s_v5 --all --query "[].{name:name,restrictions:restrictions}"

    A row with restrictions: [] means the size is sold in that region. No row means the name is wrong or not offered there. NotAvailableForSubscription or a zone restriction explains a SkuNotAvailable error: pick another size or zone, or ask support.

    az monitor activity-log list --offset 1h -g RGNAME --status Failed

    Failed control-plane operations from the last hour, with the caller and the error message. The activity log is where a failed VM create or an AuthorizationFailed shows up first.

    az vm restart -g RGNAME -n INSTANCE

    A graceful restart: the OS shuts down and starts again on the same host, with its disks and IPs. To move to a new host, use az vm redeploy, which wipes the temporary disk.

    Changes state

    Instance metadata, from inside the VM

    TOKEN=$(curl -s -X PUT http://169.254.169.254/latest/api/token -H 'X-aws-ec2-metadata-token-ttl-seconds: 60') curl -s -H "X-aws-ec2-metadata-token: $TOKEN" http://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data/instance-id

    AWS IMDSv2: a session token first, then every read sends it. A 401 means the token is missing or expired. Other paths: instance-type, placement/availability-zone, iam/security-credentials/ (the role name only; the name after it returns live keys, so do not paste it).

    curl -s -H "X-aws-ec2-metadata-token: $TOKEN" http://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data/spot/instance-action

    Empty with a 404 on a normal Spot instance. Once AWS decides to reclaim it, the body holds the action and the time, giving about two minutes' warning. Poll every 5 seconds from a drain script.

    aws ec2 modify-instance-metadata-options --instance-id INSTANCE --http-put-response-hop-limit 2 --region REGION

    A container on the instance gets no answer from the metadata address when the hop limit is 1: the token reply dies one hop short. Setting it to 2 fixes that. Adding --http-tokens required enforces IMDSv2 and breaks clients still on IMDSv1: check the MetadataNoToken CloudWatch metric first.

    Changes state

    curl -s -H 'Metadata-Flavor: Google' http://metadata.google.internal/computeMetadata/v1/instance/zone

    GCE metadata needs the Metadata-Flavor: Google header; without it the server answers 403. Other paths: instance/name, instance/service-accounts/default/email, project/project-id. 169.254.169.254 also answers.

    curl -s -H 'Metadata-Flavor: Google' http://metadata.google.internal/computeMetadata/v1/instance/preempted curl -s -H 'Metadata-Flavor: Google' http://metadata.google.internal/computeMetadata/v1/instance/maintenance-event

    TRUE or FALSE for a Spot or preemptible VM, and NONE or a maintenance event such as MIGRATE_ON_HOST_MAINTENANCE or TERMINATE_ON_HOST_MAINTENANCE. Add ?wait_for_change=true to block until the value changes.

    curl -s -H 'Metadata:true' --noproxy '*' 'http://169.254.169.254/metadata/instance?api-version=2021-02-01'

    Azure IMDS: the Metadata:true header is required, and the request must bypass any proxy, so --noproxy. Fields: compute.vmSize, compute.zone, compute.resourceGroupName, network.interface.

    curl -s -H 'Metadata:true' --noproxy '*' 'http://169.254.169.254/metadata/scheduledevents?api-version=2020-07-01'

    Planned maintenance for this VM: Reboot, Redeploy, Freeze, Preempt or Terminate, each with a NotBefore time. An empty Events list means nothing is planned.

    Service health

    • AWS: Health Dashboard for public events. Events for your own resources are in the same console, signed in.
    • Google Cloud: Status dashboard for public incidents. Personalized Service Health in the console lists the ones that touch your projects.
    • Azure: Azure status for public incidents. Service Health in the portal lists the ones that touch your subscriptions.

    Cloud CLI errors

    An error occurred (ExpiredToken) when calling the GetCallerIdentity operation: The security token included in the request is expired

    Temporary credentials (an assumed role, SSO or an instance role session) ran out. Get new ones: aws sso login or re-assume the role. Stale AWS_SESSION_TOKEN in the environment keeps failing after you fix the profile: unset it.

    The SSO session associated with this profile has expired or is otherwise invalid. To refresh this SSO session run aws sso login with the corresponding profile.

    The cached SSO login is gone. Run aws sso login with the same --profile in a terminal with a browser, or add --use-device-code over SSH.

    An error occurred (AccessDenied) when calling the ListBuckets operation: User: arn:aws:iam::123456789012:user/ops is not authorized to perform: s3:ListAllMyBuckets because no identity-based policy allows the s3:ListAllMyBuckets action

    The ending names the layer that denied it. "because no identity-based policy allows": add an Allow. "with an explicit deny in a service control policy": an Organizations guardrail, ask the org admin. "permissions boundary", "session policy" and "resource-based policy" read the same way.

    An error occurred (UnauthorizedOperation) when calling the RunInstances operation: You are not authorized to perform this operation. User: arn:aws:iam::123456789012:user/ops is not authorized to perform: ec2:RunInstances ... Encoded authorization failure message: ENCODED

    EC2 hides the reason in the encoded message. Decode it with aws sts decode-authorization-message --encoded-message ENCODED --query DecodedMessage --output text. That needs the sts:DecodeAuthorizationMessage permission.

    An error occurred (RequestLimitExceeded) when calling the DescribeInstances operation (reached max retries: 2): Request limit exceeded.

    EC2 throttled your account's API calls: a loop, a monitoring agent or several tools polling. Other services say ThrottlingException or Throttling with "Rate exceeded". Back off, and set AWS_RETRY_MODE=adaptive and AWS_MAX_ATTEMPTS=10.

    An error occurred (InsufficientInstanceCapacity) when calling the RunInstances operation (reached max retries: 2): We currently do not have sufficient m5.large capacity in the Availability Zone you requested

    AWS has no spare hardware of that type in that zone right now. Retry later, pick another zone or instance type, or omit the zone. A stopped instance may fail to start for the same reason: try a different type.

    An error occurred (VcpuLimitExceeded) when calling the RunInstances operation: You have requested more vCPU capacity than your current vCPU limit of 0 allows for the instance bucket that the specified instance type belongs to.

    A quota, not capacity: the account's vCPU limit for that instance family. InstanceLimitExceeded and MaxSpotInstanceCountExceeded are the older and Spot versions. Check with get-service-quota, then request an increase.

    ERROR: (gcloud.compute.instances.list) You do not currently have an active account selected.

    No signed-in account. Run gcloud auth login, or gcloud auth activate-service-account with a key file for automation. gcloud auth list shows what is stored.

    ERROR: (gcloud.compute.instances.create) Could not fetch resource: - Quota 'CPUS' exceeded. Limit: 24.0 in region us-central1.

    A project quota is full: the quoted metric, limit and region say which. Compare with gcloud compute regions describe REGION. Delete idle instances or request an increase in IAM and Admin, Quotas.

    The zone 'projects/PROJECT_ID/zones/ZONE' does not have enough resources available to fulfill the request.

    Google's ZONE_RESOURCE_POOL_EXHAUSTED: no capacity of that machine type in that zone. Try another zone or machine type, or retry later. A reservation holds capacity in advance.

    ERROR: Please run 'az login' to setup account.

    The Azure CLI has no valid session, or its token expired. Run az login, or az login --use-device-code over SSH. For automation, sign in with a service principal or a managed identity.

    The client '...' with object id '...' does not have authorization to perform action 'Microsoft.Compute/virtualMachines/read' over scope '...' or the scope is invalid. If access was recently granted, please refresh your credentials.

    The role assignment is missing at that scope, or the scope is mistyped, often the wrong subscription. The action names the permission to grant. A new assignment can take a few minutes: az logout and az login refreshes the token.

    The requested size for resource '...' is currently not available in location '...' zones '...' for subscription '...'. Please try another size or deploy to a different location or zones.

    Azure's SkuNotAvailable: that VM size is restricted for your subscription in that region or zone. List the restrictions with az vm list-skus, then pick another size or zone.

    Operation could not be completed as it results in exceeding approved standardDSv5Family Cores quota. Additional details - Deployment Model: Resource Manager, Location: eastus, Current Limit: 10, Current Usage: 8, Additional Required: 4, (Minimum) New Limit Required: 12.

    A vCPU quota is full in that region, for one VM family or for the regional total. Current Limit and Current Usage are the numbers from az vm list-usage. File a quota request in the portal under Usage + quotas.

    Terraform and OpenTofu

    Infrastructure as code breaks in a few places: a state lock nobody released, a plan that shows changes nobody made (drift), a lock file or provider that does not install, and a backend that was changed under the working directory. Commands run in the root module's directory (the one holding the .tf files and .terraform), as the identity that owns the backend credentials. Output is from Terraform 1.17 and OpenTofu 1.13; tofu takes the same commands and flags, and the entries say where it differs. Look first, then change.

    Terraform: look first

    terraform version

    The core version and, after init, each installed provider with its version. A core older than the required_version in the code fails with "Unsupported Terraform Core version". Compare it with the version your pipeline runs.

    terraform workspace show

    The active workspace, default unless someone created another. A plan that wants to create everything is often the right code in the wrong workspace: run this before you panic.

    terraform state list

    Every resource address the state tracks. Empty output with a non-empty configuration means a fresh or wrong backend, workspace or schema, not an empty estate. Compare with terraform workspace list and the backend block.

    terraform plan -detailed-exitcode

    Exit code 0 is no changes, 1 an error, 2 changes pending. Use it in a check job to alarm on drift. The plan text is the same as plain plan.

    Terraform: state locks

    terraform plan -lock-timeout=60s

    Waits up to a minute for the state lock instead of failing at once. A run that applies for longer than that holds the lock the whole time, so a long wait is a real run, not a stuck one. Read the Who and Created lines first.

    cat .terraform.tfstate.lock.info

    Local backend only: the lock holder as JSON (ID, Operation, Who, Created). The lock itself is an operating system file lock held by the process, so a killed run leaves this file behind but holds no lock: the next plan or apply runs and removes it. Remote backends keep the lock in the backend.

    terraform force-unlock LOCKID

    Removes the state lock whose ID the error printed. If that run is still alive, two writers can corrupt the state, so confirm the holder is gone first (CI job dead, pod gone). Backends differ: the local one says "LocalState not locked", and the pg backend reports success yet changes nothing, because its lock is a session advisory lock that frees itself when the holder's connection closes.

    Can lose data

    terraform plan -lock=false

    A plan that skips the lock, for a read when a stuck lock blocks you. It writes nothing for a plain plan. Never use -lock=false on apply: it removes the protection that keeps two runs apart.

    Terraform: drift

    terraform plan -refresh-only

    Lists only what changed outside Terraform (a deleted or edited remote object), under "Objects have changed outside of Terraform", and proposes no remote change. Use it to see drift on its own before a normal plan mixes it with code changes.

    terraform apply -refresh-only

    Writes the observed values into the state and changes no remote object. Accepting a deletion this way makes Terraform forget the resource, so the next plan wants to create it again: that is the intended result of an accepted drift.

    Changes state

    terraform plan -replace=ADDR

    Shows the destroy-and-create of one resource without running it. The plan says "must be replaced" and marks the reason. Prefer this over the old taint, which edits state.

    terraform apply -replace=ADDR

    Destroys the resource and creates it again in one run. Data on it is lost and downstream resources may be replaced too: read the plan's "Plan: N to add, N to change, N to destroy" line before you approve.

    Can lose data

    terraform plan -out=FILE

    Saves the exact plan. Apply it with terraform apply FILE, which skips the approval prompt. If the state changes after the plan was made, the apply fails with "Saved plan is stale": plan again. terraform show FILE prints it.

    Terraform: state surgery

    terraform state show ADDR

    The attributes Terraform holds for one instance. Before any state command that writes, save a copy with terraform state pull > FILE. A wrong or missing address fails with "No instance found for the given address!" Use state list to copy the exact address, including ["key"] for for_each.

    terraform state mv ADDR NEWADDR

    Renames a resource in the state only; it does not edit the code. Until the code uses the new address, the next plan wants to destroy the new one and create the old. Prefer a moved block, which travels with the code.

    Changes state

    cat > moved.tf <<'EOF' moved { from = random_pet.a to = random_pet.b } EOF

    A moved block records a rename in the code. The plan shows "(moved from random_pet.a)" and no destroy, and the state updates on the next apply. Keep it until every environment has applied, then delete it.

    Changes state

    cat > import.tf <<'EOF' import { to = ADDR id = "ID" } EOF

    Brings an existing remote object under management: the resource block must already exist in the code. plan shows "1 to import" and apply records it. A resource that does not support import fails with "Resource Import Not Implemented".

    Changes state

    terraform state rm ADDR

    Makes Terraform forget a resource without touching the remote object. The next plan wants to create it again, which can fail on a name clash or duplicate the object. Use it only to hand an object to other code. A removed block with lifecycle { destroy = false } does the same from code and shows in the plan first.

    Can lose data

    terraform state push -force FILE

    Overwrites the stored state with a saved copy (see state pull). Without -force Terraform refuses an older copy with "cannot import state with serial 3 over newer state with serial 4". Anything applied since the copy was taken is forgotten, so run plan before anything else.

    Can lose data

    Terraform: providers and the lock file

    terraform init -upgrade

    Re-selects the newest provider and module versions the constraints allow and rewrites .terraform.lock.hcl. A provider major version can change behaviour, so read the plan afterwards. Commit the lock file with the change.

    Changes state

    Terraform: backends and workspaces

    terraform init -reconfigure

    Reads the backend block fresh and ignores the old backend setup. It does not copy state. If the new location is empty, state list is empty and the next plan wants to create everything: that is the signal you pointed at the wrong place.

    Changes state

    terraform init -migrate-state

    Copies the state from the previously configured backend to the new one, asking before it does. Without a terminal it stops with "Error asking for confirmation: EOF": add -force-copy in a pipeline. Check state list on the new backend before you delete the old one.

    Changes state

    terraform workspace select WORKSPACE

    Switches the active workspace. Fails with Workspace "..." doesn't exist. for an unknown name; add -or-create to create it, or use workspace new.

    Changes state

    terraform workspace delete WORKSPACE

    Deletes the workspace's state. A workspace that still tracks resources is refused with "Workspace is not empty"; -force deletes the state and leaves the remote objects running with nothing managing them.

    Can lose data

    Terraform: debugging and OpenTofu

    TF_LOG=DEBUG TF_LOG_PATH=tf.log terraform plan

    Writes debug logs to a file; levels are TRACE, DEBUG, INFO, WARN, ERROR. The log can hold tokens and resource attributes, so do not post it as is. Look for the first [ERROR] and the provider's RPC lines.

    read -rs pass export TF_ENCRYPTION="key_provider \"pbkdf2\" \"k\" { passphrase = \"$pass\" } method \"aes_gcm\" \"m\" { keys = key_provider.pbkdf2.k } state { method = method.aes_gcm.m }" tofu plan

    OpenTofu state encryption (OpenTofu 1.7 and later), set here through the TF_ENCRYPTION variable so the passphrase never reaches a file. An encrypted state needs the same configuration on every run, including state list and plan; a wrong passphrase gives "decryption failed for all provided methods", and a state that is still plain text needs an unencrypted fallback first (see the errors).

    Terraform errors

    Error: Error acquiring the state lock Error message: resource temporarily unavailable Lock Info: ID: 9d4990f5-acc2-db53-b653-77e134a38573 Path: terraform.tfstate Operation: OperationTypeApply Who: root@93841bbafcc4 Version: 1.17.2 Created: 2026-11-20 21:01:19.980727194 +0000 UTC Info:

    Another run holds the lock. Who and Created say whose and since when, Operation what it is doing. Wait or use -lock-timeout; unlock only when the holder is gone. A killed run on a remote backend that keeps a lock record (not pg, whose lock frees when the connection closes) leaves a stale lock: this block's Created time tells you.

    Failed to unlock state: LocalState not locked

    force-unlock on the local backend when no process holds the lock. Nothing is stuck: local locks vanish with their process, even when .terraform.tfstate.lock.info is left behind. A lock you cannot clear belongs to a remote backend.

    Error: Inconsistent dependency lock file The following dependency selections recorded in the lock file are inconsistent with the current configuration: - provider registry.terraform.io/hashicorp/random: required by this configuration but no version is selected

    The lock file is missing, or does not match the required_providers constraints. Run terraform init (add -upgrade after you changed a version constraint) and commit the new lock file.

    Error: Required plugins are not installed The installed provider plugins are not consistent with the packages selected in the dependency lock file: - registry.terraform.io/hashicorp/random: there is no package for registry.terraform.io/hashicorp/random 0.0.1 cached in .terraform/providers

    .terraform does not hold the locked provider, usually a fresh checkout or a cache from another working directory. Run terraform init; in CI cache .terraform/providers keyed by the lock file.

    Error: Failed to install provider Error while installing hashicorp/random v3.9.1: the current package for registry.terraform.io/hashicorp/random 3.9.1 doesn't match any of the checksums previously recorded in the dependency lock file

    The downloaded provider does not match the lock file's checksums: another platform than the one that wrote the lock, a changed mirror, or a tampered package. Record every platform with providers lock; treat a mismatch on the same platform as a security event.

    Error: Failed to query available provider packages Could not retrieve the list of available versions for provider hashicorp/time: no available releases match the given constraints >= 99.0.0

    No release satisfies the version constraint (or the registry or mirror cannot be reached: the text then names the network error). Run terraform providers to see which module sets the constraint.

    Error: Backend initialization required, please run "terraform init" Reason: Initial configuration of the requested backend "pg"

    The backend block changed, or .terraform is missing. Run init: use -migrate-state to carry the state to the new backend, or -reconfigure to start from what the new backend already holds.

    Error: Cycle: random_pet.c1 random_pet.c2

    Resources reference each other directly or through others, so no order exists. The lines list the loop. Remove one reference or use a variable or a data source. terraform graph draws it.

    Error: Invalid for_each argument on fe.tf line 2, in resource "time_sleep" "fe": 2: for_each = toset([random_pet.new.id]) │ random_pet.new.id is a string, known only after apply

    for_each (or count) uses a value Terraform learns only at apply. Key the instances by values known at plan time, or apply the dependency first with -target, then run a normal plan.

    Error: Saved plan is stale The given plan file can no longer be applied because the state was changed by another operation after the plan was created.

    The state changed after plan -out. Plan again and apply the new file. In a pipeline this means two runs raced: add a lock or serialise the jobs.

    Error: Resource already managed by Terraform Terraform is already managing a remote object for time_static.t. To import to this address you must first remove the existing object from the state.

    An import target already holds an object. Check state show; state rm first only when you mean to replace what is tracked there.

    Error: Resource Import Not Implemented This resource does not support import. Please contact the provider developer for additional information.

    The provider's resource has no import support (random_pet is one). Recreate it, or manage the existing object with a different resource type.

    Error: Unsupported Terraform Core version This configuration does not support Terraform version 1.17.2. To proceed, either choose another supported Terraform version or update this version constraint.

    The required_version constraint excludes the binary that ran. Use the pinned version (a .terraform-version or CI image) instead of loosening the constraint.

    Error: No value for required variable The root module input variable "need" is not set, and has no default value.

    A variable without a default got no value, and -input=false (as in CI) cannot prompt. Pass -var, -var-file or a TF_VAR_need variable. A wrong type gives "Invalid value for input variable".

    Provider produced inconsistent result after apply

    The provider returned a value that disagrees with the plan, and the message ends "This is a bug in the provider". The object usually exists: run plan again to see the real state, then report it to the provider and upgrade or pin another version. From Terraform's source.

    Provider produced inconsistent final plan

    Between plan and apply the provider changed the planned action or returned an invalid new value ("produced an invalid new value for ..."). It is a provider bug: re-run the apply, since the second plan often settles, then update the provider.

    Error: Error acquiring the state lock Error message: failed to write backup file: encountered unencrypted payload without unencrypted method configured

    OpenTofu with state encryption on, reading a state that is still plain text. The first apply that migrates needs a fallback pointing to an unencrypted method; remove that fallback once the state is encrypted.

    Error: Error acquiring the state lock Error message: failed to write backup file: decryption failed for all provided methods attempted decryption failed for state: decryption failed: cipher: message authentication failed

    OpenTofu: the passphrase or key does not decrypt this state. Use the key that wrote it. Despite the heading, nothing is locked: the lock step is where the state is first read.

    Error: Error refreshing the state Failed to load state: Unsupported state file format: This state file is encrypted and can not be read without an encryption configuration

    OpenTofu read an encrypted state without the encryption block or TF_ENCRYPTION. Set it in the environment of every command, including tofu state list. A passphrase under 16 characters fails with "passphrase is too short (minimum 16 characters)".

    Vault and OpenBao

    Vault problems come as a sealed node, a cluster without a leader, a token that cannot do what it needs, a lease that will not go away, or an audit device that stops every request. Commands run on a node or a workstation with the CLI as vault (bao for OpenBao: same commands and output, with BAO_ADDR and BAO_TOKEN). Set VAULT_ADDR and take the token without a trace in history: read -rs VAULT_TOKEN; export VAULT_TOKEN. Tested on Vault 2.2 and OpenBao 2.7 with Raft storage; names below are Vault's.

    Vault: look first

    vault status

    Exit code 0 is unsealed, 2 sealed or uninitialised, 1 cannot connect. Sealed true needs an unseal; HA Mode says active or standby, Active Node Address names the leader, and Removed From Cluster true means this node was taken out of the Raft cluster. Seal Type transit or awskms means auto-unseal.

    printenv | grep -E '^(VAULT_ADDR|VAULT_CACERT|VAULT_NAMESPACE|BAO_ADDR|BAO_CACERT|BAO_NAMESPACE)='

    The settings that point the CLI at a server. A stale VAULT_ADDR (a load balancer, an old node, http against https) explains most "it works on that machine". The token is left out on purpose.

    curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}\n' "$VAULT_ADDR/v1/sys/health"

    The load balancer's view. 200 active, 429 standby, 472 DR secondary, 473 performance standby, 501 not initialised, 503 sealed, 530 removed from the Raft cluster (Vault 2.2: checked). 472 and 473 exist only with Vault Enterprise replication; OpenBao returns none of 472, 473 and 530. A balancer must route to 200, or pass ?standbyok=true. See the health API.

    vault operator raft list-peers

    Raft members with State (leader or follower) and Voter. A new node shows follower and false for the first seconds while autopilot stabilises it. Needs a token: without one, "permission denied". A sealed node answers "Vault is sealed".

    vault operator raft autopilot state

    Healthy, Failure Tolerance (nodes you can lose and keep a quorum) and per node Healthy, Last Contact and Last Index. A node with a growing Last Contact is unreachable or stuck. Failure Tolerance 0 means one more loss ends the cluster.

    vault read sys/leader

    is_self, leader_address and leader_cluster_address. It needs no token, and every node answers from its own view, so ask each node to find one that disagrees; leader_address empty means no leader. On a standby active_time reads 0001-01-01T00:00:00Z.

    journalctl -u vault --since "30 min ago" --no-pager | grep -E '\[(ERROR|WARN)\]'

    The service log filtered to problems (the unit is vault, openbao for OpenBao). Raft and seal trouble show here before they show in the API. Without systemd use docker logs or the server's stderr.

    Vault: unseal and seal

    vault operator unseal

    Prompts for one unseal key share (hidden). Repeat with different shares until Unseal Progress reaches the threshold. A key that is not valid base64 fails with "must be a valid hex or base64 string"; shares that do not combine fail with "invalid key" and progress resets.

    Changes state

    vault operator init

    Initialises a new, empty cluster and prints the unseal (or recovery) keys and the first root token once, with default shares and threshold. Do not send the output to a log or a ticket; use -recovery-pgp-keys or -pgp-keys to get encrypted shares. An initialised cluster answers "Vault is already initialized". Revoke the root token afterwards.

    Changes state

    vault operator seal

    Seals this node: it stops serving, and on the active node the cluster elects another. Sealing every node is an outage until someone unseals, or auto-unseal does it. Use it for a suspected compromise, not for a restart.

    Can lose data

    vault operator step-down

    Asks the active node to give up leadership so a standby takes over. Clients see a brief pause. Use it before you restart or patch the active node.

    Changes state

    Vault: Raft cluster

    vault operator raft remove-peer NODEID

    Removes a server from the Raft configuration. A live node removed this way logs "node has been removed from the HA cluster", shuts its core down and shows Removed From Cluster true; it must be wiped and rejoined. Remove only a node that is gone for good. It needs a leader: with a majority of voters lost it fails with "local node not active but active cluster node not found", and the way out is the peers.json recovery below.

    Can lose data

    sudo systemctl stop vault && sudo sh -c 'echo "[{\"id\":\"NODEID\",\"address\":\"HOST:8201\",\"non_voter\":false}]" > DATADIR/raft/peers.json' && sudo systemctl start vault

    Last resort when most voters are lost for good: on every surviving node (stop them all first), write peers.json with only the survivors, then start. Vault logs "raft recovery initiated: recovery_file=peers.json", deletes the file and elects a leader from that list. Use one entry per survivor, take a snapshot copy of the data first, and rebuild the lost nodes as new joiners.

    Can lose data

    vault operator raft snapshot save FILE

    Writes a point-in-time copy of the cluster's data (run it on the active node, with a token that may read it). Take one before any peer removal, upgrade or restore. snapshot inspect FILE lists the contents.

    Vault: tokens and policies

    vault token lookup

    The calling token: policies, ttl, expire_time, explicit_max_ttl, renewable, orphan, num_uses. An expired or revoked token cannot look itself up and gets "permission denied"; a ttl of 0 belongs to root and other tokens that do not expire.

    vault token lookup -accessor ACCESSOR

    The same fields for another token, found by its accessor (the id is not needed and is not shown). Use it to see who owns a token a log line names. display_name and path say which auth method created it.

    vault token capabilities VPATH

    What the calling token may do on that path: read, create, update, delete, list, sudo, deny, or root. For kv version 2 use the data/ path (secret/data/app/x), not the path you type with vault kv get. deny is the answer behind "permission denied".

    vault token capabilities -accessor ACCESSOR VPATH

    The same check for another token, without putting its secret on a command line. Compare with the policy text from vault policy read VPOLICY: path globs (*) match a prefix only, and + matches one segment.

    vault token renew

    Resets the calling token's TTL from now: to its period for a periodic token, else to its creation TTL or the -increment asked for. A renew cannot pass the explicit_max_ttl or the mount's max TTL: Vault caps it with the warning "TTL of ... exceeded the effective max_ttl". An app that renews at the cap needs a new login, not more renews.

    Changes state

    vault token revoke -accessor ACCESSOR

    Revokes a token and every child token and lease below it, so dynamic credentials issued to it stop too; only children that were created as orphans survive (-mode=orphan on a token keeps all children). The holder gets "permission denied" at once. Look it up first.

    Can lose data

    Vault: leases

    vault lease lookup LEASEID

    ttl, expire_time, issue_time and renewable of a secret lease (a dynamic credential). A revoked or expired lease says "invalid lease". The ID is the full path, such as database/creds/ro/ and an ID.

    vault list sys/leases/lookup/VPATH

    The lease IDs below a prefix such as database/creds/ro. Many entries mean clients ask for credentials faster than they release them: look at the role's TTL and at the app's cache.

    vault lease renew LEASEID

    Extends one lease, up to the role's max TTL. The CLI prints the new lease_duration; "lease not found" means it is already gone. Add -increment=5m for an exact request, which the engine may shorten.

    Changes state

    vault lease revoke LEASEID

    Queues revocation of one lease: the engine deletes the dynamic credential (the database user). "All revocation operations queued successfully!" does not say it finished; run lease lookup to see "invalid lease".

    Changes state

    vault lease revoke -prefix VPATH

    Revokes every lease under a path, such as database/creds/ro, and kills every credential in use there. Apps need new credentials at once. Use it after a leak, not as housekeeping.

    Can lose data

    vault lease revoke -force -prefix VPATH

    Removes the lease records even when the engine cannot delete the real credential (the database is down). Vault can then forget users that still exist in the database. Use it only to clear leases for an engine that is gone, and clean up the targets by hand.

    Can lose data

    Vault: audit and TLS

    vault audit list -detailed

    The audit devices and their options. With devices enabled, Vault refuses every request when none of them can write the entry. If this command also fails with "internal error", an audit device is blocked: see the errors group.

    vault audit enable file file_path=FILE

    Adds a file audit device. Enable a second device on another disk before you take the first out of service: with at least one working device Vault keeps serving. The path must be writable by the Vault user.

    Changes state

    openssl s_client -connect HOST:PORT -servername NAME </dev/null | openssl x509 -noout -subject -issuer -dates -ext subjectAltName

    The certificate the listener presents: subject, issuer, notAfter and the DNS and IP names it covers. A name the client uses that is not in the alternative names gives an x509 "valid for" error, and an issuer your client does not trust gives "unknown authority".

    Vault errors

    Error checking seal status: Get "https://127.0.0.1:8200/v1/sys/seal-status": http: server gave HTTP response to HTTPS client

    VAULT_ADDR says https and the listener has TLS off (tls_disable = 1). Fix the scheme. The mirror image is "Client sent an HTTP request to an HTTPS server." with code 400.

    Error checking seal status: Get "https://127.0.0.1:8200/v1/sys/seal-status": tls: failed to verify certificate: x509: certificate signed by unknown authority

    The CLI does not trust the listener's CA. Set VAULT_CACERT to the CA file; do not use VAULT_SKIP_VERIFY outside a quick test. A host not in the certificate names gives "certificate is valid for ... not ...".

    Error checking seal status: Get "http://127.0.0.1:9/v1/sys/seal-status": dial tcp 127.0.0.1:9: connect: connection refused

    Nothing listens at that address: the service is down, the port is wrong, or Vault is still starting (it opens the listener only after the seal provider answers). Check systemctl status vault and the log.

    Code: 503. Errors: * Vault is sealed

    The node is sealed, so every API call fails. Run vault status, then unseal, or fix the auto-unseal provider if it is a transit or KMS seal. A restart seals a Shamir node again.

    Code: 400. Errors: * Vault is already initialized

    vault operator init on a cluster that has data. Nothing is wrong. Wiping storage and initialising again destroys all secrets: restore from a snapshot instead.

    Code: 403. Errors: * 1 error occurred: * permission denied

    The token is valid but its policies do not allow that path and operation. Run vault token capabilities VPATH and vault token lookup, then fix the policy. Kv version 2 needs the data/ path in the policy. "2 errors occurred: permission denied, invalid token" means the token itself is expired or revoked; "bad token" means a malformed one.

    Code: 400. Errors: * invalid key: failed to compute combined key: all parts must be the same length

    Shares from different keys, a mistyped share or another cluster's key were mixed. Progress resets after this error: start the unseal over with correct shares. A wrong share is not rejected until the threshold is reached.

    Code: 404. Errors: * no handler for route "auth/nosuch/login". route entry not found.

    No auth method or engine is mounted at that path. vault auth list and vault secrets list show the mounts; the mount path is not always the type name.

    Code: 403. Errors: * preflight capability check returned 403, please ensure client's policies grant access to path "nomount/foo/"

    vault kv get on a path whose mount does not exist, or that the token cannot see. The wording blames policy even for a mount typo: check vault secrets list first.

    WARNING! The following warnings were returned from Vault: * Invalid path for a versioned K/V secrets engine. See the API docs for the appropriate API endpoints to use. If using the Vault CLI, use 'vault kv get' for this operation.

    vault read on a kv version 2 mount. Use vault kv get, or read secret/data/PATH and write policies against data/. "No value found at secret/data/..." means the path is valid and empty.

    Code: 400. Errors: * invalid lease

    A lease lookup for a lease that was revoked, expired or never existed. lease renew says "lease not found" for the same case. Request new credentials.

    Code: 500. Errors: * internal error

    On a node with a working seal, a 500 on every request, even vault audit list, points at the audit device. The log shows the cause (next entry). On its own it can also be a storage problem: read the log.

    [ERROR] core: failed to audit request: path=auth/token/lookup-self | error writing file for sink "/aud/audit.log": unable to re-write to file for sink "/aud/audit.log": write /aud/audit.log: no space left on device

    The only audit device cannot write, so Vault refuses all requests rather than serve unlogged. Free space or fix permissions on the audit path. Enable a second device on another disk to avoid a single point of failure.

    Code: 500. Errors: * local node not active but active cluster node not found

    This node is a standby and knows no active node, so it cannot forward. The cluster has no leader: a quorum of voters is down or cut off. In the log: "failed to contact quorum of nodes, stepping down". Restore the network or the voters.

    [ERROR] core: shutting down core: error="node has been removed from the HA cluster"

    An operator or autopilot removed this node from the Raft configuration. vault status shows Removed From Cluster true. Stop it, wipe its data directory and rejoin with vault operator raft join if it should come back.

    error parsing Seal configuration: Put "http://vault-seal.example.test:8200/v1/transit/encrypt/unseal": dial tcp: lookup vault-seal.example.test on 127.0.0.11:53: no such host

    Auto-unseal through transit could not reach the seal provider at startup, so the node cannot start. Restore the name resolution or network to the seal server, or check the KMS credentials and key for other seal types.

    App runtimes: JVM, Node.js, Python, Go

    What breaks inside the process: a heap that fills, threads stuck on a lock, a loop that pins a core, or a limit set from the host instead of the container. Take a thread or goroutine dump first; a restart throws the evidence away.

    JVM

    jcmd

    Lists the running JVMs with their process id and main class. Run it as the same user as the JVM, and in the same container: a JVM from another user or mount namespace does not show up.

    jcmd PID Thread.print

    A thread dump. Look at each thread's java.lang.Thread.State: many BLOCKED threads waiting on the same lock, or RUNNABLE threads in the same method, show the bottleneck. Take three dumps, five seconds apart, to see what does not move. Deadlocks are reported at the end. jstack PID is the older name.

    kill -3 PID

    Asks the JVM to print a thread dump to its own stdout (the container log), without stopping it. Use it when there is no jcmd, for example in a JRE-only image. The JVM does not exit.

    Changes state

    jcmd PID GC.heap_info

    Heap by generation: used against total per space, and Metaspace. Old generation (tenured or old) at 90 percent or more after a collection means a leak or a heap that is too small.

    jstat -gcutil PID 1000 5

    A GC sample every second, five times. O is old-generation percent used, YGC and FGC the young and full collection counts, GCT total GC seconds. O stuck near 100 with FGC climbing: the JVM is about to throw an OutOfMemoryError.

    jcmd PID GC.class_histogram | head -20

    The classes using the most heap, by instances and bytes. It forces a full collection first (the GC log says "Heap Inspection Initiated GC"), so expect a pause on a big heap. The top entry is usually the leak: [B is byte[], [C is char[].

    jcmd PID GC.heap_dump FILE

    Writes a heap dump (hprof) for Eclipse MAT or VisualVM. It pauses the JVM for the duration, and the file is about the size of the live heap. FILE must be writable by the JVM's user, and the dump holds every secret in memory: guard it.

    Changes state

    jcmd PID VM.native_memory summary

    Native memory by category: Java Heap, Class, Thread, Code, GC, Internal. It only works if the JVM started with -XX:NativeMemoryTracking=summary; otherwise it says "Native memory tracking is not enabled". Use it when RSS far exceeds the heap. baseline then summary.diff shows growth.

    jcmd PID JFR.start name=triage duration=60s filename=/tmp/triage.jfr

    Starts a Java Flight Recorder recording, about 1 percent overhead, that stops by itself. Read it with jfr summary /tmp/triage.jfr or jfr print --events jdk.ExecutionSample /tmp/triage.jfr. Check with jcmd PID JFR.check; dump a running one with JFR.dump.

    Changes state

    jcmd PID VM.flags jcmd PID VM.command_line

    The flags the JVM really runs with, including the ergonomic ones: MaxHeapSize, the collector in use, and every -X option. Read these before changing any: a JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS in the environment shows here too.

    java -XX:+PrintFlagsFinal -version | grep -E 'MaxHeapSize|MaxRAMPercentage|UseSerialGC|UseG1GC'

    What a fresh JVM picks on this host or container. In a 512 MiB container it chose a 128 MiB heap (MaxRAMPercentage defaults to 25) and the Serial collector, because the JVM treats under 1792 MB or fewer than 2 CPUs as a small machine. A -Xmx overrides the percentage.

    java -XX:MaxRAMPercentage=75 -XX:+ExitOnOutOfMemoryError -XX:+HeapDumpOnOutOfMemoryError -XX:HeapDumpPath=/tmp -jar app.jar

    The container-aware sizing: the heap is 75 percent of the container limit, which leaves room for Metaspace, thread stacks and buffers. A heap near 100 percent of the limit gets the container OOM-killed with no Java error. The last two flags write a dump on failure, then exit so the orchestrator restarts it.

    java -Xlog:gc*,safepoint:file=/var/log/app/gc.log:time,uptime,level,tags:filecount=5,filesize=20m -jar app.jar

    A rotating unified GC log (JDK 9 and later; the old -XX:+PrintGCDetails is gone). Read the Pause lines: pauses over a second, or "Pause Full" repeating, mean GC is the bottleneck. The directory must exist and be writable.

    ps -o nlwp= -p PID

    The thread count of a process. A count that grows without bound is a thread leak and ends in "unable to create native thread". Compare with cat /sys/fs/cgroup/pids.max in a container (cgroup v2).

    JVM errors

    Exception in thread "main" java.lang.OutOfMemoryError: Java heap space

    The heap is full and a collection could not free enough. A leak (heap dump, then the histogram) or a heap smaller than the load needs (-Xmx or MaxRAMPercentage). The stack under it is only where the last allocation failed, not the cause.

    Exception in thread "main" java.lang.OutOfMemoryError: Metaspace

    Class metadata filled -XX:MaxMetaspaceSize. A classloader leak, usually from redeploys, or code generating classes without end (proxies, scripting). Count loaders with jcmd PID VM.classloader_stats. Metaspace is native memory, not the heap.

    Exception in thread "main" java.lang.OutOfMemoryError: unable to create native thread: possibly out of memory or process/resource limits reached

    The OS refused a new thread: not the heap. Check the thread count (ps -o nlwp=), the container's pids.max, ulimit -u and free native memory. The usual cause is a pool with no upper bound. The JVM can stay up afterwards, with its other threads still running.

    Exception in thread "main" java.lang.OutOfMemoryError: GC overhead limit exceeded

    The Parallel collector spent almost all its time in GC and freed under 2 percent of the heap. It is a leak or an undersized heap that took a long way to fail. Treat it like "Java heap space".

    Terminating due to java.lang.OutOfMemoryError: Java heap space

    -XX:+ExitOnOutOfMemoryError fired: the JVM exits at the first OutOfMemoryError, so the supervisor can restart it. A heap dump written before the exit (HeapDumpOnOutOfMemoryError) is the evidence.

    Exception in thread "main" java.lang.StackOverflowError

    Recursion without an end, or a very deep call chain. The repeated frames at the top of the trace name the loop. Fix the recursion first; -Xss makes a bigger stack per thread but only delays it.

    Node.js

    node --inspect=127.0.0.1:9229 app.js

    Starts the V8 inspector: the process prints "Debugger listening on ws://127.0.0.1:9229/..." and Chrome DevTools attaches at chrome://inspect. Keep it on loopback and reach it through an SSH tunnel. The inspector has no authentication, so 0.0.0.0 hands out remote code execution.

    kill -USR1 PID

    Turns the inspector on in a running Node process, listening on 127.0.0.1:9229, with no restart. Check with curl -s http://127.0.0.1:9229/json/list. It stays on until the process exits.

    Changes state

    node --heapsnapshot-signal=SIGUSR2 app.js

    Writes a heap snapshot (Heap.*.heapsnapshot in the working directory) each time the process gets kill -USR2 PID. Load it in the DevTools Memory tab and compare two snapshots. The process pauses while it writes, and a big heap needs about as much free memory again.

    node --max-old-space-size=64 --heapsnapshot-near-heap-limit=1 app.js

    Writes a snapshot just before the heap limit is hit, so a crash that nobody could catch leaves evidence. The snapshot itself needs memory, so give a margin. Use a larger value than 64, which is only for a test.

    node -e "console.log(require('v8').getHeapStatistics().heap_size_limit / 1048576)"

    The heap limit in MB that Node picked on this host. In a 512 MiB container it printed 259. Change it with --max-old-space-size=MB or NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=MB; keep it below the container limit, because buffers and native memory sit outside the heap.

    kill -USR2 PID

    With --report-on-signal set at start, this writes a diagnostic report (a JSON file named report.*.json): JavaScript and native stacks, heap statistics, libuv handles, environment. It needs no inspector and works on a stuck process. The report can contain environment variables. Without that flag, USR2 kills the process: check ps -o args= -p PID first.

    Changes state

    node -e "const h=require('perf_hooks').monitorEventLoopDelay({resolution:20});h.enable();setInterval(()=>{console.log('p99 ms',h.percentile(99)/1e6,'max ms',h.max/1e6);h.reset()},5000)"

    Event loop delay, as p99 and max in ms. Paste the first two statements and the setInterval into the app or a --require file; run on its own it just prints zeros. Under 20 ms is healthy; hundreds of ms means a synchronous call, a big JSON parse or a CPU loop blocks every request.

    node --cpu-prof --cpu-prof-dir=/tmp/prof app.js

    Writes a .cpuprofile when the process exits. Open it in the DevTools Performance tab, or load it in a flame graph viewer. For a running process, record from the inspector instead.

    node --trace-gc app.js

    Prints a line per collection: Scavenge (young) or Mark-Compact (old), heap before and after, and the pause in ms. Mark-Compact running every second with the heap barely shrinking is a leak.

    Node.js errors

    FATAL ERROR: Reached heap limit Allocation failed - JavaScript heap out of memory

    The old space reached --max-old-space-size and a collection did not help. Above it, the "Last few GCs" lines show Mark-Compact freeing almost nothing: a leak. Take snapshots over time, or raise the limit if the working set really grew. Node aborts, so a supervisor must restart it.

    [UnhandledPromiseRejection: This error originated either by throwing inside of an async function without a catch block, or by rejecting a promise which was not handled with .catch(). The promise rejected with the reason "plain string".] { code: 'ERR_UNHANDLED_REJECTION' }

    Since Node 15 an unhandled rejection kills the process. This text appears when the reason is not an Error; with an Error, Node prints its stack instead. Find the async call with no await or .catch(). --unhandled-rejections=warn keeps the process alive; use it only as a stopgap.

    Error: listen EADDRINUSE: address already in use :::3000

    Another process, often the previous instance that did not exit, holds the port. Find it with ss -tlnp 'sport = :3000'. The code is EADDRINUSE.

    Error: connect ECONNREFUSED 127.0.0.1:3999

    The target host answered with a reset: nothing listens on that address and port. In a container, localhost is the container itself, not the host or the database service. Check the service name and port.

    Error: read ECONNRESET

    The peer closed the connection abruptly while you were using it: an idle timeout on a load balancer, a crashed server, or a keep-alive connection reused after the server dropped it. For HTTP clients it often shows as socket hang up. Retry idempotent calls, and keep the client's keep-alive shorter than the server's.

    RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded

    Recursion without an end, or a very large apply or spread of an array. The trace shows the same function repeating. It is a normal, catchable error in the process, not a crash.

    Python

    py-spy dump --pid PID

    Every thread's current stack without editing the program, like a thread dump. active+gil is the thread running Python code; idle threads wait. By default it pauses the process while it reads the stacks; add --nonblocking to never pause it, at the cost of an occasional torn stack. It needs ptrace rights: run it as the same user or root. Add --locals for variable values. Install: pip install py-spy.

    py-spy top --pid PID

    A live view of where the time goes by function, like top. It pauses the process for every sample, 100 a second by default: add --nonblocking on a busy production process. It needs a terminal: in a container use docker exec -it. Without a terminal it fails with "Not a tty".

    py-spy record --pid PID --duration 30 --format raw -o /tmp/py.txt

    Samples for 30 seconds into folded stacks (one per line, with a count), ready for a flame graph tool. Like top, it pauses the process for every sample unless you add --nonblocking. Leave --format raw out to write an SVG flame graph. The heaviest stacks are on the lines with the largest numbers.

    PYTHONFAULTHANDLER=1 python app.py

    Turns on faulthandler: a crash from a segfault, an abort or a C extension prints every thread's Python stack to stderr, "Fatal Python error: Segmentation fault". In code, faulthandler.register(signal.SIGUSR1) dumps all stacks on kill -USR1 PID, and faulthandler.dump_traceback_later(30, repeat=True) dumps a hung process on a timer.

    python -X tracemalloc=5 app.py

    Tracks every allocation with 5 frames of stack; it costs memory and speed, so use it in a test. In code, tracemalloc.take_snapshot().statistics('lineno')[:5] prints lines such as app.py:3: size=19.9 MiB, count=20001, average=1042 B. Compare two snapshots with snapshot.compare_to(old, 'lineno').

    PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1 python app.py

    Stdout and stderr are written at once, not in blocks. Without it, a Python process in a container or a pipe can look silent for minutes while its logs sit in a buffer. Use python -u for the same effect.

    python -c "import sys; print(sys.getrecursionlimit())"

    The recursion limit, 1000 by default. A RecursionError needs real recursion or a cycle, so raise it only for a known deep structure, with sys.setrecursionlimit.

    Python errors

    RecursionError: maximum recursion depth exceeded

    A function calls itself without a stop, or two call each other. The traceback shows "[Previous line repeated 996 more times]" and the repeated frame is the loop. Fix the base case; raising the limit only moves the crash.

    MemoryError

    An allocation failed: the process hit its address-space or memory limit (ulimit -v, a container limit) or the host is out of memory. Often a huge list, a file read whole or a big bytes object. Under a cgroup limit the kernel usually kills the process instead (exit 137, "Killed"), with no Python error.

    BrokenPipeError: [Errno 32] Broken pipe

    Writing to a pipe whose reader has gone, as in python app.py | head -1, or a client that closed a socket before the reply. A script piped to head can ignore it; in a server, it means the client left. Python raises it only at write time.

    OSError: [Errno 24] Too many open files: '/etc/passwd'

    The process hit its file-descriptor limit: leaked files, sockets or connections not closed. Count them with ls /proc/PID/fd | wc -l, compare with ulimit -n and grep 'open files' /proc/PID/limits, and close with with blocks.

    Fatal Python error: Segmentation fault

    A crash in C code: a native extension, ctypes or the interpreter. With faulthandler on, the Python stack follows ("most recent call first"), and its top line is the Python call that went into C. Update the extension, or reproduce under gdb.

    Error: Failed to find python version from target process

    py-spy attached to a process that is not a Python interpreter: often the shell that started it (sh -c 'python app.py') or a wrapper. Find the real PID with pgrep -a python. In a container, start with exec python so it is PID 1.

    Error: Permission denied (os error 13)

    py-spy lacks the right to attach. Run it as root or the process's user. In Docker, start the container with --cap-add SYS_PTRACE; with a system-wide Yama setting, kernel.yama.ptrace_scope set to 1 or higher blocks attaching to non-children.

    Go

    curl -s 'http://127.0.0.1:6060/debug/pprof/goroutine?debug=1' | head -30

    Goroutines grouped by identical stack: the count at the start of each group, then the frames. The first line says goroutine profile: total N. One stack with thousands of goroutines is a leak, usually blocked on a channel or a lock nobody releases. It needs import _ "net/http/pprof" and a server on that port. Keep it on loopback: pprof exposes internals.

    curl -s 'http://127.0.0.1:6060/debug/pprof/goroutine?debug=2' | head -60

    Every goroutine with its full stack and state, in the same shape as a crash dump: goroutine 98 [running]:, [chan receive], [select (no cases)], [IO wait], [sync.Mutex.Lock]. A goroutine blocked a long time shows it, as in [chan receive, 5 minutes]. With many goroutines it pauses the program briefly.

    go tool pprof -top -nodecount=15 'http://127.0.0.1:6060/debug/pprof/profile?seconds=30'

    A 30-second CPU profile, ranked: flat is time in the function itself, cum includes what it calls. The sample total tells you how busy it was. runtime.asyncPreempt near the top is just a busy loop. Add -http=:8081 for the flame graph. Profiling costs a few percent of CPU for the 30 seconds.

    go tool pprof -top -sample_index=inuse_space -nodecount=15 http://127.0.0.1:6060/debug/pprof/heap

    Live heap by function: inuse_space is what is held now, alloc_space all that was allocated. Take two profiles minutes apart and compare with -base: the function that grows is the leak. A heap profile shows the state at the last completed collection.

    kill -QUIT PID

    Makes a Go program print every goroutine's stack to stderr, then exit with code 2: "SIGQUIT: quit". That is the dump for a hung process with no pprof, but it ends the process, so supervisors restart it. Read it from the log. A Java process, by contrast, survives kill -3.

    Changes state

    GODEBUG=gctrace=1 ./app

    One line per collection on stderr: gc 12 @4.2s 3%: ... 18->20->9 MB, 20 MB goal, 8 P. The percentage is time spent in GC so far; the three sizes are heap at start, at end and live. A live size that rises each cycle is a leak; a high percentage means too much garbage.

    GOMEMLIMIT=400MiB GOMAXPROCS=2 ./app

    A soft memory limit that makes the collector work harder near it, and the number of OS threads running Go code. Before Go 1.25, GOMAXPROCS is the host's CPU count even in a container with a CPU limit (it printed 8 with --cpus 2), so a limited container gets throttled: set it, or use the automaxprocs package. GOMEMLIMIT is soft, so keep it under the container limit.

    GOTRACEBACK=all ./app

    On a crash, print every goroutine, not only the one that failed. GOTRACEBACK=crash also raises SIGABRT for a core dump. The default prints only the failing goroutine (single).

    go version -m ./app

    The Go version a binary was built with, its module path and every dependency version. Use it to see what is actually deployed.

    Go errors

    fatal error: concurrent map writes

    Two goroutines wrote to the same map at once. Unlike a panic, this fatal error cannot be recovered; the process dies. Guard the map with sync.Mutex or sync.Map. Build with go build -race to find the lines.

    fatal error: concurrent map read and map write

    The same bug when one goroutine reads while another writes. The trace lists the goroutine that detected it, not every one involved: use the race detector to find the other.

    fatal error: all goroutines are asleep - deadlock!

    Every goroutine is blocked: a channel nobody sends to, a WaitGroup never done, a mutex locked twice. Go only detects this when none can run; a deadlock inside a server with timers is silent. The stack below shows what each waits on.

    panic: runtime error: invalid memory address or nil pointer dereference [signal SIGSEGV: segmentation violation code=0x1 addr=0x0 pc=0x466c70]

    A nil pointer, map or interface was used. addr=0x0 confirms it. The first frame of the goroutine that follows, under goroutine 1 [running]:, is the file and line where it happened.

    panic: runtime error: index out of range [5] with length 1

    A slice or array indexed past its length. The numbers say the index and the length. Find the loop or the caller that computed the index.

    panic: close of closed channel

    A channel closed twice, or closed by two goroutines. Only the sender should close, and once. panic: send on closed channel is the sibling: a sender outlived the close.

    goroutine 1 [running]: main.main() /src/app/main.go:5 +0x10 exit status 2

    The shape of every Go panic dump. goroutine N [state] is one goroutine; running is the one that panicked. Its frames read top-down from the failure to the caller, each with file:line. "created by main.main in goroutine 1" below another goroutine says who started it. Exit status 2 is a panic.

    runtime: goroutine stack exceeds 1000000000-byte limit fatal error: stack overflow

    Recursion without an end: the 1 GB maximum stack was reached, so the process needs that much memory first and may be OOM-killed before it prints. The repeating frames are the loop.

    Killed
